# Todos: a Snapback 4 web starter

A Vite + React app over a local-first Snapback 4 backend: one table, one live
query and three mutations. It works offline and syncs when the link returns.

```sh
npm install
npx snapback4 check      # compiles snapback/schema.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
npx snapback4 dev        # serves http://127.0.0.1:4400 and reloads on save
npm run dev              # in a second terminal: http://localhost:5173
```

`snapback4 init --web --port 4401` points the client at that backend and serves
the page on the next port (4402); `--web-port N` picks the page's port, which
`vite.config.mts` holds for both `npm run dev` and `vite preview`.

Choose "Continue as a guest", then add a few todos. Open a second tab to see
live updates; stop the backend to see writes queue offline and settle when it
returns.

## Files

- `snapback/schema.q`: the backend (tables, rules, `sync`, queries, mutations).
  Edit it and `snapback4 dev` re-adopts it; `snapback4 check` regenerates the API.
- `fixtures/`: seed rows and expected outcomes for `snapback4 test`.
- `src/client.ts`: one `createApp` for the whole app. This synced todo schema
  uses `device: webDevice()`; a bare `createClient()` in a browser refuses
  mutations with `E_ADMISSION`.
- `src/App.tsx`: sign-in, the live list (`usePage`) and writes (`useMutation`).
  `usePage` passes the query's cursor itself: a paged query takes `c: cursor ?`
  and reads `after c` (`npx snapback4 guide reads`); any other query reads
  with `useQuery`. Props name hook results by operation (`UsePage<typeof
  api.list>`, `UseQuery<…>`, `UseMutation<…>`), and `useSignedInViewer()` is
  the viewer below the sign-in. After you replace the schema,
  `npx snapback4 init --adapt` swaps this screen for a `Home` that compiles
  against any schema, while it is unmodified.
- `src/styles.css`, `index.html` (titled after this directory) and
  `vite.config.mts`: yours to change; `.mts` keeps Vite's config ESM even when
  package.json says `"type": "commonjs"`.

## Next steps

- Grow the schema: `npx snapback4 guide language`, then `guide rules`, `guide sync`.
- Try operations from the terminal: `npx snapback4 run add '{"title":"milk"}' --as alice`.
- Test without a server: `npx snapback4 test --fixture fixtures --as alice` loads
  `fixtures/todos.jsonl` and runs the expectations in `fixtures/test.cases.jsonl`
  (`npx snapback4 guide testing`).
- Add email accounts: change `use identity` to `use identity password` and see `guide auth`.
- Ship: `VITE_SNAPBACK_URL=https://your-backend npm run build` builds `dist/`
  with an offline app shell (`snapback4 web-shell dist`); serve it over HTTPS.
- A complete multi-screen example: `npx snapback4 init <dir> --web --template chat`.

   191	fn compose(head: &str, body: &str) -> String {
   192	    fn split(text: &str) -> (Vec<&str>, &str) {
   193	        let mut imports = Vec::new();
   194	        let mut rest = text;
   195	        while let Some(line) = rest.split_inclusive('\n').next().filter(|line| line.starts_with("import ")) {

   202	    let (body_imports, body_code) = split(body);
   203	    // (`import {` or `import type {`, module, names) in first-seen order.
   204	    let mut merged: Vec<(String, String, Vec<String>)> = Vec::new();
   205	    for line in head_imports.into_iter().chain(body_imports) {
   206	        let (Some(open), Some(close)) = (line.find('{'), line.find('}')) else { panic!("starter imports are `import {{ … }} from \"…\";`: {line}") };
   207	        let (kind, module) = (line[..open].trim().to_owned(), line[close + 1..].trim().to_owned());

   212	        }

   217	}

   177	    let mut client_leftover = None;
   178	    if wanted_sign_in != sign_in {
   179	        let (from, to) = (sign_in.label(), wanted_sign_in.label());

   181	        reasons.push(format!("the unmodified starter screen shows {from}, but {because}; adapt writes {to}"));

   183	        if let Some(port) = port_of(&client) { client_text = client_text.replace("127.0.0.1:4400", &format!("127.0.0.1:{port}")); }
   184	        client_leftover = Some(Leftover { path: client_path, why: format!("the unmodified starter client for {from}; adapt writes {to}"), rewrite: Some(client_text.clone()) });
   185	    }
   186	    if wanted_sign_in == sign_in && client_changed {
   187	        if let Some(port) = port_of(&client) { client_text = client_text.replace("127.0.0.1:4400", &format!("127.0.0.1:{port}")); }
   188	        let why = "the unmodified web client needs the device or principal journal selected by your schema's synced tables".to_owned();
   189	        reasons.push(why.clone());

   196	            (Body::Todos, _) => "your snapback/*.q declares the todo starter's operations again; adapt writes the Todos screen back".to_owned(),

   200	    let why = if why.starts_with("the unmodified") { why } else { format!("the unmodified starter screen: {why}") };
   201	    found.push(Leftover { path: app_path, why, rewrite: Some(app_text) });
   202	    found.extend(client_leftover);
   203	}

   205	/// The `api` path a starter Home's first read calls, if it has one.
   206	fn first_read(app: &str) -> Option<String> {
   207	    let after = app.split_once("function FirstRead()")?.1;
   208	    let op = after.split_once("useQuery(api.")?.1.split_once(',')?.0;
   209	    Some(op.to_owned())
   210	}
   211	
   212	fn read(dir: &Path, path: &str) -> Option<String> {
   213	    let path = dir.join(path);
   214	    path.symlink_metadata().ok().filter(|metadata| metadata.is_file())?;
   215	    std::fs::read_to_string(path).ok()
   216	}
   217	
   218	/// A starter client without what init or the schema varies: the backend port
   219	/// and the persona list.
   220	fn normalized(client: &str) -> String {
   221	    let lines = client.split_inclusive('\n').map(|line| {
   222	        if line.starts_with(PERSONAS) { format!("{PERSONAS}[];\n") } else { line.to_owned() }
   223	    }).collect::<String>();
   224	    match port_of(&lines) { Some(port) => lines.replace(&format!("127.0.0.1:{port}"), "127.0.0.1:4400"), None => lines }
   225	}
   226	
   227	const PERSONAS: &str = "export const personas: readonly string[] = ";
   228	
   229	fn personas_of(client: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   230	    client.lines().find_map(|line| line.strip_prefix(PERSONAS))
   231	        .and_then(|list| serde_json::from_str(list.trim_end().trim_end_matches(';')).ok()).unwrap_or_default()
   232	}
   233	
   234	fn port_of(client: &str) -> Option<&str> {
   235	    let at = client.find("127.0.0.1:")? + "127.0.0.1:".len();
   236	    let digits = client[at..].find(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(client.len() - at);
   237	    (digits > 0).then(|| &client[at..at + digits])
   238	}

   240	/// One `check` line, or none: the leftovers and the command that fixes them.
   241	pub(crate) fn report(dir: &Path) -> Option<String> {
   242	    let (found, advice) = leftovers(dir);
   243	    if found.is_empty() && advice.is_empty() { return None; }
   244	    let mut lines = Vec::new();
   245	    for leftover in &found { lines.push(format!("  {}: {}", leftover.path, leftover.why)); }
   246	    for line in &advice { lines.push(format!("  {line}")); }
   247	    if !found.is_empty() {
   248	        lines.push("  fix: snapback4 init --adapt (removes or rewrites only these files, which still match the starter; your own files are never touched)".into());
   249	    }
   250	    Some(format!("starter: {} left from `snapback4 init` that {} not fit your schema\n{}",
   251	        if found.is_empty() { "a screen".to_owned() } else { format!("{} file{}", found.len(), if found.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }) },
   252	        if found.len() == 1 || found.is_empty() { "does" } else { "do" }, lines.join("\n")))
   253	}
   254	
   255	/// The same, for `check --json`: `null` when nothing is left over.
   256	pub(crate) fn report_json(dir: &Path) -> serde_json::Value {

   258	    if found.is_empty() && advice.is_empty() { return serde_json::Value::Null; }
   259	    serde_json::json!({

   261	        "advice": advice,
   262	        "fix": if found.is_empty() { serde_json::Value::Null } else { "snapback4 init --adapt".into() },
   263	    })
   264	}

   266	/// `snapback4 init --adapt [dir]`.
   267	pub(super) fn adapt(dir: &Path) -> i32 {

/bin/bash -lc "git show a4b4900270:snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/adapt.rs | sed -n '1,175p'" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2

//! Starter leftovers on the init-first path: `init --web` (or `--expo`) wrote
//! the todo starter, then the project replaced `snapback/schema.q`. The
//! starter's own files that still match the bytes init wrote, but no longer
//! fit the schema, are leftovers: `check` names them and `init --adapt`
//! removes or rewrites them. A file the project changed is never touched.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.2 — leftovers are judged by the starter's original bytes, never by name alone.
use super::starter::{self, Body, Platform};
use super::{OwnSchema, own_schema};
use std::path::Path;

/// One unmodified starter file that no longer fits the project's schema.
pub(crate) struct Leftover {
    pub path: &'static str,
    pub why: String,
    /// The text `init --adapt` writes; `None` removes the file.
    pub rewrite: Option<String>,
}

/// Starter operations its fixtures, cases and `Todos` screen call.
const STARTER_OPERATIONS: [&str; 4] = ["list", "add", "toggle", "remove"];

/// Whether a schema still has the starter's `todos` table and the operations
/// its fixtures, cases and `Todos` screen call.
pub(super) fn fits_todos(own: &OwnSchema) -> bool {
    own.tables.iter().any(|table| table == "todos")
        && STARTER_OPERATIONS.iter().all(|op| own.operations.iter().any(|name| name == op))
}

/// What the schema in `dir` leaves of the starter, and the advice that goes
/// with it (a sign-in the project must write by hand). No schema: nothing.
pub(crate) fn leftovers(dir: &Path) -> (Vec<Leftover>, Vec<String>) {
    let Ok(Some(own)) = own_schema(dir) else { return (Vec::new(), Vec::new()) };
    let mut found = Vec::new();
    let mut advice = Vec::new();
    let todos = fits_todos(&own);
    if !todos {
        for (path, text) in starter::FIXTURES {
            if read(dir, path).is_some_and(|current| current == super::without_design_references(text)) {
                let what = if path.ends_with("todos.jsonl") { "rows for the todo starter's `todos` table" } else { "cases for the todo starter's list/add/toggle/remove" };
                found.push(Leftover { path, why: format!("the unmodified {what}; your snapback/*.q has no todos table with those operations, so `snapback4 test` would refuse it"), rewrite: None });
            }
        }
    }
    for platform in [Platform::Web, Platform::Expo] {
        screens(dir, platform, &own, todos, &mut found, &mut advice);
    }
    if let Some(text) = read(dir, "src/native-smoke.ts") {
        let wanted = starter::native_smoke(own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests));
        if text != wanted && [false, true].into_iter().any(|password| text == starter::native_smoke(password)) {
            found.push(Leftover { path: "src/native-smoke.ts", why: "the unmodified native smoke helper uses the previous schema's sign-in".into(), rewrite: Some(wanted) });
        }
    }
    readme(dir, &own, todos, &mut found);
    app_json(dir, &mut found);
    (found, advice)
}

/// The starter README, while it is still one init wrote (for either screen,
/// at the ports it names), when it describes another screen than the schema
/// now gets: the Todos README beside a project's schema, or the reverse.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (042): adapt rewrote the screen but left a README about todos and guest sign-in.
fn readme(dir: &Path, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
    let platform = if dir.join("src/App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Web } else if dir.join("App.tsx").is_file() { Platform::Expo } else { return };
    let title = super::title(dir);
    let personas = own.identity.is_none() && !own.personas.is_empty();
    let wanted_body = if todos { Body::Todos } else { Body::Home(None) };
    for path in ["README.md", "README.snapback.md"] {
        let Some(text) = read(dir, path) else { continue };
        let port = number_after(&text, "127.0.0.1:");
        let web_port = number_after(&text, "localhost:");
        let ports = [(port, web_port), (None, web_port), (port, None), (None, None)];
        // The offline paragraph names the schema's synced tables when init
        // wrote it; any of them (or 0.4.22's unconditional claim) still matches.
        let masked = starter::without_offline(&text);
        let matched = ports.into_iter().find(|&(port, web_port)| {
            [(Body::Todos, false), (Body::Home(None), false), (Body::Home(None), true)].iter()
                .any(|(body, personas)| [false, true].iter().any(|password| masked == starter::without_offline(&super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, body, *personas, &starter::Offline::Unknown, *password)), port, web_port))))
        });
        let Some((port, web_port)) = matched else { continue };
        let wanted = super::localize(path, &super::without_design_references(&starter::identity_readme(platform, &title, &wanted_body, personas, &own.offline, own.identity.is_some_and(|(password, guests)| password && !guests))), port, web_port);
        if wanted == text { continue; }
        let about = if todos { "the todo starter's operations, which your snapback/*.q declares again" } else { "your snapback/*.q and the Home screen, named after this directory" };
        let only_offline = !text.starts_with("# Todos") && starter::without_offline(&wanted) == masked;
        // A schema that does not compile right now says nothing about what it syncs.
        if only_offline && own.offline == starter::Offline::Unknown { continue; }
        let why = if only_offline {
            "the unmodified starter README says what works offline for another schema; adapt rewrites that paragraph from the tables your snapback/*.q syncs".to_owned()
        } else {
            format!("the unmodified starter README describes {}; adapt rewrites it to describe {about}", if text.starts_with("# Todos") { "the todo starter (its todos table and guest sign-in)" } else { "the Home screen for another schema" })
        };
        found.push(Leftover { path, why, rewrite: Some(wanted) });
    }
}

/// An Expo `app.json` that still carries the todo starter's slug, scheme and
/// bundle ids (written by 0.4.21 and earlier): adapt names them after the
/// directory and changes nothing else in the file.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — wave 6 (067): adapted Expo apps kept dev.snapback4.todos.
fn app_json(dir: &Path, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>) {
    let Some(text) = read(dir, "app.json") else { return };
    let Ok(config) = serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(&text) else { return };
    let expo = &config["expo"];
    let [(_, slug), (_, scheme), (_, bundle)] = starter::TODO_IDS;
    if expo["slug"] != slug || expo["scheme"] != scheme || expo["ios"]["bundleIdentifier"] != bundle || expo["android"]["package"] != bundle { return; }
    let (new_slug, new_scheme, new_bundle) = starter::app_ids(&super::title(dir));
    let quoted = |value: &str| format!("\"{value}\"");
    let rewrite = text.replace(&quoted(bundle), &quoted(&new_bundle)).replace(&quoted(slug), &quoted(&new_slug)).replace(&quoted(scheme), &quoted(&new_scheme));
    found.push(Leftover { path: "app.json", why: format!("the todo starter's ids (slug {slug}, scheme {scheme}, bundle id {bundle}); adapt names them after this directory ({new_slug}, {new_scheme}, {new_bundle})"), rewrite: Some(rewrite) });
}

fn number_after(text: &str, prefix: &str) -> Option<u16> {
    let at = text.find(prefix)? + prefix.len();
    let digits: String = text[at..].chars().take_while(char::is_ascii_digit).collect();
    digits.parse().ok()
}

/// The sign-in a schema admits, as the starter screen it needs: the guest
/// screen (`None`), the persona picker (`Some(personas)`), or no starter
/// screen fits (`Err(advice)`).
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum SignIn { Guest, Password, Personas(Vec<String>) }
impl SignIn {
    fn label(&self) -> String {
        match self { Self::Guest => "the guest sign-in".into(), Self::Password => "the password sign-in".into(), Self::Personas(names) => format!("a persona picker ({})", names.join(", ")) }
    }
    fn personas(&self) -> Option<&[String]> { match self { Self::Personas(names) => Some(names), _ => None } }
}
fn wanted(own: &OwnSchema) -> Result<SignIn, &'static str> {
    match own.identity {
        Some((_, true)) => Ok(SignIn::Guest),
        Some((true, false)) => Ok(SignIn::Password),
        None if !own.personas.is_empty() => Ok(SignIn::Personas(own.personas.clone())),
        _ => Err("declares neither `use identity` nor `use personas`, so nobody can act: add `use identity` for the guest sign-in or `use personas alice, bob` for development, then rerun `snapback4 init --adapt`"),
    }
}

fn screens(dir: &Path, platform: Platform, own: &OwnSchema, todos: bool, found: &mut Vec<Leftover>, advice: &mut Vec<String>) {
    let (app_path, client_path) = platform.paths();
    let (Some(app), Some(client)) = (read(dir, app_path), read(dir, client_path)) else { return };
    // Which starter screen is on disk, if both files are still as init wrote
    // them: its sign-in (guest, or a persona picker) and its body.
    let journal_shape = starter::Offline::Tables { synced: vec![], total: 0 };
    let client_shape = normalized(&client);
    let matches_client = |reference: &str, personas: bool| [reference, starter::previous_client(platform, personas)].iter().any(|reference|
        client_shape == normalized(reference) || client_shape == normalized(&starter::web_client((*reference).to_owned(), &journal_shape)));
    let current = [Body::Todos, Body::Home(first_read(&app))].into_iter().find_map(|body| {
        let (guest_app, guest_client) = starter::screens(platform, None, &body);
        let (persona_app, persona_client) = starter::screens(platform, Some(&[]), &body);
        let (password_app, password_client) = starter::identity_screens(platform, None, &body, true);
        if app == guest_app && matches_client(&guest_client, false) { Some((SignIn::Guest, body)) }
        else if app == password_app && matches_client(&password_client, false) { Some((SignIn::Password, body)) }
        else if app == persona_app && matches_client(&persona_client, true) { Some((SignIn::Personas(personas_of(&client)), body)) }
        else { None }
    });
    let Some((sign_in, body)) = current else { return };
    // A Home keeps its read while the schema still declares that query
    // argument-free; a Home without one stays as written (it fits any schema).
    let wanted_body = match &body {
        _ if todos => Body::Todos,
        Body::Home(None) => Body::Home(None),
        Body::Home(Some(op)) if own.reads.contains(op) => body.clone(),
        _ => Body::Home(own.reads.first().cloned()),
    };
    // A sign-in no starter screen fits keeps the one on disk, with advice.
    let wanted_sign_in = match wanted(own) {
        Ok(wanted) => wanted,
        Err(why) => {
            advice.push(format!("{app_path} is the starter's {}, but your snapback/*.q {why}", sign_in.label()));
            sign_in.clone()
        }
    };
    let (app_text, mut client_text) = starter::identity_screens(platform, wanted_sign_in.personas(), &wanted_body, wanted_sign_in == SignIn::Password);
    client_text = starter::web_client(client_text, &own.offline);
    let client_changed = client_shape != normalized(&client_text);
    if wanted_sign_in == sign_in && app == app_text && wanted_body == body && !client_changed { return; }

### 7.6 Wave 7 (0.4.22): what works offline, and one flag for both ports

- **The README's offline sentence follows the schema (R3, 5 React lanes:
  004 038 064 082 091; three named it first).** 0.4.22 made the README
  describe the project's schema but kept the todo starter's "It works
  offline and syncs when the link returns", so an all-`online only` app's
  README (and init's "offline-capable" summary) promised offline reads it
  does not have (064: "the only observed misleading behavior"; 091: "a trust
  issue"). The Home README now carries one paragraph, starting "Offline", chosen
  from the compiled schema: no synced table ("reads need the server; writes
  made offline are kept on this device and sent when the link returns"),
  every table synced, or the synced tables by name; a schema that does not
  compile yet gets the general rule (tables that declare `sync` work
  offline). init's summary says the same in a clause. `init --adapt` (and
  `check`'s leftover report) recognizes an unmodified starter README whatever
  its offline paragraph says, including 0.4.22's sentence, and rewrites only
  that paragraph when the schema's synced tables changed; a schema that does
  not compile right now never triggers that rewrite. The todo starter's own
  README keeps its claim: its one table syncs. The Expo opening no longer
  says device builds "work offline" unconditionally either.
- **`--port N` is the one flag (11 lanes; 027 054 068 079 091 asked for
  it).** `init --web --port N` already wrote the client URL, the README's
  `dev --port N` and the Vite dev and preview port N+1, but init's line named
  only `--web-port`. Without `--port`, the line now says "`init --web --port
  N` sets the backend to N and the page to N+1 together" (Expo: the client
  URL and README together), and `init --help` describes `--port` that way.
- **The first read spreads `...otherwiseAll` first** (LLP 3000.031 §8: the
  spread is order-free now; first is where a copied handler is read as an
  override).

## 8. Wave 9: production onboarding and a browser proof

**Implementer:** Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-12 (DX workstream).
The commission covers wave 9 fix list #6, #7, #10, #13, #14 and the jobs
recipe in #18. REL owns the persona-to-password deploy checklist; HAND owns
protocol negotiation and hot-activation envelope consistency.

Password identity produces signup/login forms on web and Expo, retaining the
existing session-store custody. Schema-first init and unmodified-screen adapt
select the password form; edited screens remain application-owned. Both
starters honor runtime `?api=` on web. Expo chooses the existing device-less
journal on web when no table syncs, preserving nativeDevice on native builds.
Expo `build:web` clears Metro on every export so a changed public URL is
compiled; Vite builds again from its current environment.

`check` emits advisory N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER for application tables lacking
an insert/upsert operation or blocked by positive bootstrap dependencies.
Insert rules and read-through parents are evaluated over a least fixed point
of potentially populated tables; jobs write as system, service-seat branches
are potential roots. Negated and unknown comparisons remain potentially
satisfiable: this is a sound conservative warning for the covered positive
fragment, not a general satisfiability proof. Native jobs are opaque and suppress
this absence analysis. A warning points to `guide deploy --section first admin`.
No rule, planner, admission or outbox changes are made. Fixture argument drift
is checked without execution with the existing wire decoder and argument
validator; saved-result references remain unknown. Neither note fails check.

`snapback4 witness web [dist]` serves an existing export on an allocated
loopback port, launches headless Chromium (SNAPBACK4_CHROMIUM overrides cache
and standard-path discovery), opens the real page with `?api=&witness=web`,
then uses its persona or sign-in UI (signup on the owned empty backend). It calls that app's authenticated SDK
client, using the first generated durable mutation or `--mutation` and `--args`, and
requires terminal `sent`; failed/pending/timeout are nonzero with a reason.
The app registers its client and generated API with
`registerWebWitness(app, api)` from `snapback4/witness-web`; starters include
this hook. It exists only under the explicit query flag, adds no authority,
and is callable by the page's own scripts already holding that client.
This proves exported client/auth/write integration, not the application's
composer UI or an arbitrary user journey. Missing registration refuses.
A fresh browser and an owned `dev --memory --port 0 --no-watch` make default
runs repeatable without changing the project's durable store. `--url` never
resets a caller-owned server; a deterministic id derived from principal,
operation selection and canonical arguments deduplicates repeated proof
writes under that store's receipt guarantees. Different arguments are new
intents. Credentials are supplied through a named environment variable,
never printed. Cleanup records the owned backend immediately at spawn, even before readiness;
its supervisor PID also stops it if the witness dies. It closes the browser/static
server and only the owned backend; long polls never require networkidle.

`upgrade [version]` detects npm/bun/pnpm by packageManager then lockfile,

// One resolver for the CLI shim and the Node device opener.
import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
import process from "node:process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

export function resolveBinary(): string {
  const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
  const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);
  // @ref LLP 3000.021#3.3 — the installed platform package precedes checkout fallbacks
  const platform = `snapback4-${process.platform}-${process.arch}`;
  const candidates: string[] = [];
  if (process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN) candidates.push(resolve(process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN));
  try { candidates.push(join(dirname(require.resolve(`${platform}/package.json`)), "snapback4")); } catch { /* not installed for this platform */ }
  candidates.push(resolve(here, "../../../target/release/snapback4"), resolve(here, "../../../target/debug/snapback4"));
  const binary = candidates.find((path) => existsSync(path));
  if (!binary) throw new Error(`snapback4: no binary for ${process.platform}-${process.arch}. Packages exist for darwin-arm64 and linux-x64; elsewhere, build with \`cargo build --release -p snapback4\` and set SNAPBACK4_BIN.`);
  return binary;
}

 succeeded in 0ms:
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

# LLP 3000.008.002.000: Common client patterns that preserve user intent

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Accepted
**Systems:** Snapback 4 client write lifecycle; React hooks and application starters; read and acquisition boundaries; receipts; media viewers; ephemeral activity integration; `check` residency notes; the client card
**Author:** Codex / GPT-6 (revision 1); Claude Opus 5.5 (revision 2, folding three direction reviews) — for Charlie Cheever
**Date:** 2026-09-26
**Revised:** 2026-10-07 (UA round 3: backoff retains the interrupted wait diagnosis across React batching, §6); 2026-10-07 (UA round 2: sealed/frozen response conversion, strict stream diagnostic order and fifteen-second unknown-progress notice, §6); 2026-10-07 (UA round 1: public body/conversion error boundary, progress-aware diagnostics and loading-literal compatibility, §6); 2026-10-07 (UA: native Blob conversion and named bounded wait diagnostics, §6); 2026-09-29 (AV round 3: size-aware opaque transfers and detecting empty-chunk regression, §6); 2026-09-29 (AV round 2: progress-sensitive stalls and admission cooldown custody, §6); 2026-09-29 (AV round 1: bounded safe downloads, recovery and effect lifetimes, §6); 2026-09-29 (AV: ordinary author-avatar recipe, render-time handle ownership and calibration d-02 reproduction limits, §6); 2026-09-26 (landed on main as `992cec75cb`; the full blocking suite on that commit had 0 failures; not in 0.4.3, which was cut from `8442e53ede`); 2026-09-26 (revision 3: folds the blind code reviews of the integration — Opus, Grok and Astra, all READY WITH FIXES — and reconciles with `main`'s `receipts` card and LLP 3000.019, which now own the receipt recipe, the outbox view, read retention and the `check` note; §4, §5, §7, §9, §10); 2026-09-26 (revision 2: folds a blind three-family direction check — Claude Opus 5.5 max, Grok 4.7 xhigh, GPT-6 Astra max, all RIGHT WITH CORRECTIONS. The Clique receipt rule is inverted back to the brief; the read gaps are traced to the client's own prediction fence; the first target moves to the published paved path and the Expo tier; one SDK boundary, `MutateOptions.waitSeconds`, is added; the author commissioned implementation, §7); 2026-09-26 (revision 1: initial proposal grounded in the published 0.4.2 Clique run)
**Related:** [LLP 3000.008.002](./3000.008.002-complete-client-experience.rfc.md) (parent, implemented increment unchanged), [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md), [LLP 3000.011.003.000](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md) (implemented starters), [LLP 3000.013](./3000.013-quarry-states-intent.rfc.md) (§13 client admission), [LLP 3000.016](./3000.016-following-on-a-bounded-device.rfc.md) (§2.2, the query publication rules), [LLP 3000.017](./3000.017-ephemeral-tables.rfc.md) (owns ephemeral semantics), [LLP 1001.000](./1001.000-ephemeral-latest-state.spec.md) (historical channel precedent); [LLP 3000.019](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md) (the same run's builder-written code; owns read retention, the outbox view and `N_SYNC_ORDER`); direction reviews: [brief](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.brief.md), [Opus](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.opus.md), [Grok](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.grok.md), [Astra](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.astra.md); code reviews: [brief](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.code.brief.md), [Opus](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.code.opus.md), [Grok](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.code.grok.md), [Astra](./reviews/3000.008.002.000-common-client-patterns.code.astra.md)

## Summary

Make the ordinary client implementation preserve a user's draft, admitted
write, navigation and media interaction through temporary read gaps,
reconnects and component remounts. Do this without retaining unauthorized
results, inventing successful settlement, or building a second outbox.

Revision 1 was right about the principles and the responsibility split, and
wrong about three facts that would have aimed the work at the wrong place
(§10 records the reviews). First, its one concrete receipt rule inverted the
benchmark brief. Second, most of the "temporary read gaps" were produced by the
client's own prediction fence, not by the network. Third, the builder in the
evidence worked from the package README, `CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md` and the
`guide` cards on the Expo tier, and several of its mistakes were copied from
those published examples. The web starter the first revision targeted already
has most of these patterns.

So the increment is: correct the paved docs and add the missing `guide client`
entry point; name the fence where it bites (the `reads` card, and a `check`
note where one can be precise); give admission its own boundary in the SDK;
and bring the Expo starter up to the web starter's per-intent outcomes. Runtime
guarantees stay in the SDK, presentation in starters, receipt policy in the
application schema. No read-retention law changes here.

## 1. Evidence and attribution

Evidence is calibration 21 on published `snapback4@0.4.2` and
`snapback4-expo@0.4.2`, one Astra-medium builder. The retained packet is
`/Users/ccheever/clique-sb4-20260925-astra-cal21/` on the black M5 Max.
Within `lanes/astra-snapback4/`, the primary sources are `diary.md`,
`run-log.md`, `final-report.md`, `grading/grader-report.json` and the
application itself (`src/`, `app/`, `snapback/`). The ledger preserves the
observations if that packet is unavailable; it does not replace it.

| Observation | Evidence locator | Responsibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| UI confused a pending mutation return with terminal success/refusal | M1 diary; run log 1790385801 | Paved composer (§3) |
| The composer held Send busy until settlement | run log 1790389016; C&O `PendingSend` disabled on `send.pending` | Published example taught it (§3) |
| Profile/auth gate unmounted every route on a transient `loading` | run log 1790386882; C&O onboarding router; README profile sample | Published example taught it (§4) |
| Acknowledgement effects repeated when `usePage.items` emptied | run log 1790386760; C&O `InboxList` renders the `[]` of a loading page | Prediction fence + published example (§4, §5) |
| A thread produced transient `loading` (the diary blamed its order; a nested point read past the horizon withholds, an order mismatch alone does not — §4) | M6 diary; `why` fixed it | Prediction fence; no `check` warning (§4) |
| Receipt shaping over horizon-limited rows caused local unavailability | run log 1790389016 | Prediction fence (§4) |
| Unread totals past the horizon hid the whole cold-offline inbox | run log 1790400140; `evidence/unread-inbox-why.txt` | Decoration in its own query (§4) |
| One live thread read per conversation to send `delivered` | `src/Chat.tsx`; deferring it until acquisition took cold acquisition 69.9 s → 5.6 s at 100 conversations (run log 1790400287, `final-report.md`) | Recipe (§5) |
| A view-once image disappeared when its list row unmounted | M5 diary; run log 1790388135 | Viewer ownership that still observes revocation (§6) |
| A 105-line app-side intent journal (`src/Pending.ts`) shown beside predicted rows, labelling an unknown receipt "failed … not queued" | run log 1790400667; `Pending.ts:42-48` | Paved per-intent outcomes (§3); SDK view candidate (§9) |
| `E_ID_REUSED` on a media retry | `scripts/replay.mjs`; `evidence/m6-suite-run2.txt:12` | The raw-HTTP replay adapter, not the composer; SDK was correct |
| Per-keystroke writes extended typing lifetime | run log 1790387112; the `reads` card taught a durable typing table | LLP 3000.017 (§6) |
| `guide client` printed "no card named client" | `evidence/guide-client.txt`; C&O first read at M5 | Missing entry point (§7) |
| Grader d-01: aggregate advanced when an unread member left | `grading/trace.md` d-01 | **Grader misreading** — the brief says leavers drop out (§5) |
| Grader d-02: `acknowledge` returned `sent` for a non-member while changing nothing | `grading/trace.md` d-02 | Application schema: refuse, don't silently filter (§5) |

The builder attributed 28 corrections to itself and established no platform
capability gap. That attribution is faithfully recorded, but it is not a
sufficient assignment of responsibility: seven of the rows above were taught by
a published example, a missing entry point or the fence, and one (d-01) was a
grading error. The final holistic score was 92/100; client integration 86,
queries 88. Those narrative scores are not acceptance criteria.

## 2. What already exists, and what must not change

Orientation snapshot: `origin/main` at `0badac9872`, plus LLP 3000.017 on
`sb4/eph-integrate`. The paved client surface is
[CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS](../snapback4/packages/snapback4/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md).
It already exposes `useMutation`, `usePendingWrite(op, id)`, `client.write(id)`,
`onWrite`, `useAcquisition()` and `useNow(deadline)`. Same-id retries with
changed input are refused (`E_WRITE_ID_REUSE`), and a missing aggregate is
already `loading`, never zero. `createApp` owns admission/recovery journals;
the local device owns its outbox. Predicted rows carry `pending: true`. The web
starter (`init --web`) already saves write ids before dispatch, observes each
intent separately, distinguishes unknown outcomes and opens rooms by a held
lookup instead of creating them.

These laws stay: `sent` with `replayed: true` carries no historical result; an
evicted receipt is unknown, never failed; no fresh id to recover a lost
acknowledgement; account isolation; loading is not empty; no protected-result
cache. The query publication rules of LLP 3000.016 §2.2 (Rules 1–5 in
`local-query.ts`) are documented here, not relaxed (§4, §9).

## 3. Submit once, then let the user continue

The composer separates three events: durable local admission (custody),
authoritative settlement, and observation in the authorized read.

- **Admission is its own boundary.** A device's `mutate()` returned only at
  settlement or after 4 s online, although admission took milliseconds (Astra's
  probe: admitted at 2 ms, returned at 4,002 ms), so a composer that awaited it
  stalled on a slow link. `MutateOptions.waitSeconds` (0–25, clamped like
  `ActOptions`) bounds that wait; `0` returns at custody with `pending` or with
  the refusal that prevented custody. Omitted keeps each tier's default.
  Custody is the device outbox, `createApp`'s online intent journal, or the
  online client's memory queue. The React write hooks take the same options:
  `run(args, { waitSeconds: 0 })`, and `run(args, { id })` for an id saved first.
- Clear the field at submit, as chat apps do, so the submitted text never
  remains and text typed meanwhile is plainly new. If the outcome is `failed`
  (nothing was kept), give the text back: into the field when it is still empty,
  otherwise on the refusal row with **Edit**, which appends rather than
  overwrites. A save that fails before dispatch says "Not sent: could not save it
  on this device; your draft is kept." (Revision 2's "clear exactly the
  submitted revision after custody" deleted a retyped draft that happened to
  start with the same text — Astra's code review; §10.) Send is disabled only
  while that submission is being admitted — never because an earlier write is
  `pending`, which lasts until terminal settlement.
- Display the admitted intent immediately. Predicted rows carry `pending: true`
  ("Sending…"). A write that queues without a row has a `reason`; show a
  labelled pending item, never a fabricated server row.
- **Per-intent outcomes.** `useMutation`'s `last` is the latest call's outcome
  by design, so a refusal of an earlier send disappears (Astra reproduced it in
  Chromium against the Expo starter's single `usePendingWrite(api.send)`). The
  paved form saves `{ id, … }` in account-and-backend-scoped storage before
  dispatch and observes each with `usePendingWrite(op, savedId)`: `failed` shows
  the refusal with the saved text to edit; a retry is a new intent with a new id;
  `sent` leaves the list, and the row carries it. `usePendingWrite` reports
  `restoring` while it looks up a saved id's recorded outcome, so a row reads
  "Saving…" while its own submission is admitted and "Checking…" while it is
  looked up; only then is a missing receipt shown as unknown — with its text and
  Dismiss, never resent.
- Correlate presentation by a declared identity, never by matching text or
  time. Pending presentation and saved drafts follow the account/store isolation
  contract; an account switch cannot display another account's text.

This remains composition plus one SDK boundary and one hook field
(`restoring`). An SDK view over the durable outbox (pending and refused intents
with their arguments) would remove the saved-intent journal from every app; LLP
3000.019 P3(4) proposes it (§9); it is not built here.

## 4. Stable UI ownership, honest read boundaries

**The client's own fence produces most read gaps.** While this device holds a
predictable pending write to a table a query reads — or a write whose predicted
footprint is unknown, which blocks every query — the query asks no server
(`local-query.ts` `blocked()`/`recover()`). What the screen then shows depends on
the device's own answer. An answer the device **withholds** stays `loading`
(test "rule 3: engine-withheld prediction"); a **partial** answer stays local
partial data (`complete: false`). What makes the device withhold a whole answer
was measured on real devices (lane L3, §7): a streaming total or extremum
(`count`, `sum`, `min`, `max` over a domain or a page) whose input crosses the
sync horizon, and a point read — a quoted message, a joined row — that misses
past the horizon. An order that differs from the window's order alone yields
partial data. Writes to unrelated tables hold nothing. A local observation
keeps its previous answer only during a server fetch, and drops it at any local
commit; every local commit also re-runs live reads from `loading` (LLP 3000.019
§3.3). `usePage` then returns `items: []`. So an app's own typing,
acknowledgement and send writes blanked its own screens, and effects in list
rows re-fired. The `reads` card said the opposite ("an unavailable ordinary
device read asks the server while online even with pending writes"), and
`check` named no affected query. This RFC does not relax the fence — LLP
3000.019 P3 proposes keeping answers across same-authority commits — and ranks
prevention above survival:

1. **Prevent.** Keep a screen's query one the device can complete: no total,
   extremum or point lookup over data past the sync horizon inside it (check with
   `snapback4 why <query>`). Put a server-needing decoration — an unread total
   over history past the horizon, a quoted message older than the horizon — in
   its own query, so the screen's list never waits on it. The `jobs` card's own

3284-
3285-`snapback4 guide <word>` prints a card on stdout for every word a builder
3286-reaches for. Aliases map to their card: `prediction`, `predict`, `offline`
3287-and `optimistic` go to a new `prediction` card; `pagination` and `cursor` go
3288-to `reads`; `migration` goes to schema evolution; `sql` goes to the inspection

3291-what makes a mutation predictable, with compiled examples:
3292-- held, horizon and server sites, with `why <op>` and `check --residency`;
3293-- a client-minted id already dedupes, so an `exists t[id]` guard past the
3294-  horizon only costs prediction;
3295-- `upsert` predicts an update and never an insert for a row the device lacks;
3296-- presence decides only effects (§19.1).

impl Platform {
    /// Where the starter writes its screen and its client.
    pub(super) fn paths(self) -> (&'static str, &'static str) {
        match self { Platform::Web => ("src/App.tsx", "src/client.ts"), Platform::Expo => ("App.tsx", "src/client.ts") }
    }
}

/// The screen a signed-in viewer sees: the starter's `Todos`, which calls
/// list/add/toggle/remove, or `Home`, which compiles against any schema (the
/// viewer and the generated api's operation names, for the project to replace),
/// with one real read when the schema has a query that takes no required
/// arguments (its `api` path, `notes` or `module.notes`).
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — a starter screen must typecheck against the schema beside it.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#6.6 — wave 5: the adapted Home renders one useQuery through matchRead, the idioms Todos taught.
#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Debug)]
pub(super) enum Body { Todos, Home(Option<String>) }

/// The starter's screen and client for a schema: the guest sign-in, or, with
/// `personas`, the persona picker, around `body`. The texts are as the
/// starter ships them, before init's port rewrite.
pub(super) fn screens(platform: Platform, personas: Option<&[String]>, body: &Body) -> (String, String) {
    let (guest, guest_client, persona, persona_client, todos, home) = match platform {
        Platform::Web => (include_str!("starter/web/guest.tsx"), include_str!("starter/web/client.ts"), include_str!("starter/personas/web-app.tsx"), include_str!("starter/personas/web-client.ts"), include_str!("starter/web/todos.tsx"), include_str!("starter/web/home.tsx")),
        Platform::Expo => (include_str!("starter/expo/guest.tsx"), include_str!("starter/expo/client.ts"), include_str!("starter/personas/expo-app.tsx"), include_str!("starter/personas/expo-client.ts"), include_str!("starter/expo/todos.tsx"), include_str!("starter/expo/home.tsx")),
    };
    let first_read = match platform { Platform::Web => include_str!("starter/web/first-read.tsx"), Platform::Expo => include_str!("starter/expo/first-read.tsx") };
    let (head, client) = match personas {
        None => (guest, guest_client.to_owned()),
        Some(personas) => {
            let list = personas.iter().map(|name| serde_json::to_string(name).unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
            (persona, persona_client.replace("PERSONAS", &format!("[{list}]")))
        }
    };
    let app = match body {
        Body::Todos => compose(head, todos),
        Body::Home(op) => {
            let (head, home) = if personas.is_some() { (persona_head_home(head), persona_home(home)) } else { (head.replace("<Todos />", "<Home />"), home.to_owned()) };
            match op {
                None => compose(&head, &home.replace("    <FirstRead />\n", "")),
                Some(op) => compose(&compose(&head, &home), &first_read.replace("api.OP", &format!("api.{op}"))),
            }
        }
    };
    (super::without_design_references(&app), super::without_design_references(&client))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.013.000#17 — both identity modes choose the no-device journal when nothing syncs.
pub(super) fn web_client(client: String, offline: &Offline) -> String {
    if matches!(offline, Offline::Tables { synced, .. } if synced.is_empty()) {
        client.replace("import { webDevice } from \"snapback4/web\";\n", "")
            .replace("tier === \"web\" ? webDevice() : ", "")
            .replace("device: webDevice(), ", "").replace(", device: webDevice()", "")
            .replace("each with its own device and store", "each with its own principal write journal")
            .replace("web uses the wasm device", "web uses the principal write journal (no synced tables)")
    } else { client }
}

/// Behind the persona picker there is no session: "Sign out" (`auth.logout()`)
/// showed "Signed out" and then a "Choose a persona" button, two steps for a
/// switch (wave 5 lane 062). The persona Home takes the picker's `leave` and
/// says "Switch persona"; the guest Home keeps its sign-out.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.10 — the adapted persona Home switches persona; it never signs out.
fn persona_head_home(head: &str) -> String {
    let leave = ["() => setPersona(null)", "() => choose(null)"].into_iter().find(|leave| head.contains(&format!("<Account leave={{{leave}}}><Todos /></Account>")))
        .expect("the persona heads render <Account leave={…}><Todos /></Account>");
    head.replace(&format!("<Account leave={{{leave}}}><Todos /></Account>"), &format!("<Account leave={{{leave}}}><Home leave={{{leave}}} /></Account>"))
}

fn persona_home(home: &str) -> String {
    let replacements = [
        ("function Home() {", "function Home({ leave }: { leave: () => void }) {"),
        ("  const auth = useAuth(), link = useLink();", "  const link = useLink();"),
        (r#"<Button title="Sign out" onPress={() => void auth.logout()} />"#, r#"<Button title="Switch persona" onPress={leave} />"#),
        (r#"<button onClick={() => void auth.logout()}>Sign out</button>"#, r#"<button onClick={leave}>Switch persona</button>"#),
        ("// Never null here: Account renders this screen only once signed in.", "// Never null here: Account renders this screen only once the persona's app is open."),

pub(super) const EXPO_ENV: &str = include_str!("starter/expo-env.d.ts");

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — password identity gets an executable sign-in screen.
pub(super) fn identity_screens(platform: Platform, personas: Option<&[String]>, body: &Body, password: bool) -> (String, String) {
    if !password { return screens(platform, personas, body); }
    let head = match platform { Platform::Web => include_str!("starter/web/password.tsx"), Platform::Expo => include_str!("starter/expo/password.tsx") };
    let (_, client) = screens(platform, None, body);
    let body_source = match (platform, body) {
        (Platform::Web, Body::Todos) => include_str!("starter/web/todos.tsx").to_owned(),
        (Platform::Expo, Body::Todos) => include_str!("starter/expo/todos.tsx").to_owned(),
        (platform, Body::Home(op)) => {
            let (home, first) = match platform { Platform::Web => (include_str!("starter/web/home.tsx"), include_str!("starter/web/first-read.tsx")), Platform::Expo => (include_str!("starter/expo/home.tsx"), include_str!("starter/expo/first-read.tsx")) };
            match op { Some(op) => compose(home, &first.replace("api.OP", &format!("api.{op}"))), None => home.replace("    <FirstRead />\n", "") }
        }
    };
    (compose(&head.replace("<Todos />", if matches!(body, Body::Todos) { "<Todos />" } else { "<Home />" }), &body_source), client)
}

pub(super) fn identity_readme(platform: Platform, title: &str, body: &Body, personas: bool, offline: &Offline, password: bool) -> String {
    let mut text = readme(platform, title, body, personas, offline);
    if platform == Platform::Expo && matches!(offline, Offline::Tables { synced, .. } if synced.is_empty()) {
        text = text.replace("web uses the wasm\ndevice", "web uses the principal write journal (no synced tables)")
            .replace("web uses the wasm device", "web uses the principal write journal (no synced tables)")
            .replace("or `webDevice()` (web)", "or the principal write journal (web, no synced tables)");
    }
    if password { text.replace(GUEST_SIGN_IN, "Sign up or sign in with email and password (`use identity password`); the SDK\nkeeps your session across restarts. See `snapback4 guide client --section password`.")
        .replace("Choose \"Continue as a guest\", then add", "Sign up or sign in with email and password, then add")
        .replace("Choose \"Continue as a\nguest\", then add", "Sign up or sign in with email and password, then add") } else { text }
}

/// Exact client templates emitted by 0.4.24, before runtime URL/witness additions.
/// Matching complete known bytes preserves the rule that edited files stay owned.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — upgrading an untouched starter still adapts its identity.
pub(super) fn previous_client(platform: Platform, personas: bool) -> &'static str {
    match (platform, personas) {
        (Platform::Web, false) => include_str!("starter/previous/web-client.ts"),
        (Platform::Web, true) => include_str!("starter/previous/web-persona-client.ts"),
        (Platform::Expo, false) => include_str!("starter/previous/expo-client.ts"),
        (Platform::Expo, true) => include_str!("starter/previous/expo-persona-client.ts"),
    }
}

// The smoke's identity choice is generated with the screen, not assumed guest.
pub(super) fn native_smoke(password: bool) -> String {
    let guest = include_str!("native-smoke.ts");
    if !password { return guest.to_owned(); }
    guest.replace("import { smokeSegment }", "import { smokeAccount, smokeSegment }")
        .replace("void auth.guest();", "const account = smokeAccount();\n    if (account) void auth.signup(account.email, account.password);")

import { registerWebWitness } from "snapback4/witness-web";
import { api } from "../snapback/generated/api.ts";
import { createApp, type App } from "snapback4/app";
import { webDevice } from "snapback4/web";

// The backend `snapback4 dev` serves. Set VITE_SNAPBACK_URL for a deployment.
export const url = (new URLSearchParams(window.location.search).get("api") ?? import.meta.env.VITE_SNAPBACK_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:4400").replace(/\/$/, "");

// Your schema declares `use personas` and no `use identity`, so nobody signs
// in: on `snapback4 dev` an app acts as a declared persona (`dev:alice`) with
// no session. The url must be loopback, and `serve` refuses personas: add
// `use identity` and a sign-in screen before you deploy (`snapback4 guide personas`).
export const personas: readonly string[] = PERSONAS;

// One app per persona, each with its own device and store; close it on switch.
export const openApp = (persona: string): App => {
  const app = createApp({ url, persona, device: webDevice() });
  registerWebWitness(app, api);
  return app;
};

// The index card (LLP 3000 §2; LLP 2000.000 §8). These are the only words a
// screen may need. The mechanism is built to fit them; a screen that needs a
// word not here is a defect in the mechanism, not a gap in the card.

export interface Session {
  readonly principal: string;
  /** `guest` or `user` for built-in identity; an application-defined kind
   * for a session minted by its service. */
  readonly kind: string;
  readonly token: string;
  readonly expiresAt: number;
  /** Absent on legacy persisted sessions; authority always comes from the server. */
  readonly scope?: "full" | "read";
  readonly capExpiresAt?: number;
}

export type SignIn = { readonly ok: true; readonly session: Session } | { readonly ok: false; readonly why: Refusal };
export type SessionRevocation = { readonly ok: true; readonly revoked: number } | { readonly ok: false; readonly why: Refusal };

/** An opaque page position minted by whoever evaluated the page. Pass it back unchanged. */
export type Cursor = string & { readonly __cursor: "Cursor" };
/** The authenticated subject a rule sees as `viewer`. */
export type Principal = string & { readonly __principal: "Principal" };

export interface Refusal {
  readonly code: string;

import {spawnSync} from 'node:child_process';
import {stripTypeScriptTypes} from 'node:module';
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
const source=(commit,file)=>spawnSync('git',['show',"'`${commit}:${file}`],{encoding:'"'utf8'}).stdout;
const src=file=>source('a4b4900270',file);
const load=async code=>import('data:text/javascript;base64,'+Buffer.from(stripTypeScriptTypes(code)).toString('base64'));
let counter=0;
globalThis.reviewMint=()=>String(++counter);
const {settleMutation}=await load(src('snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/write-settlement.ts').replace('import { mintId } from \"./client.ts\";','const mintId = globalThis.reviewMint;'));
const {sentResult,matchRead,otherwiseAll}=await load(src('snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts'));
const recipes=src('snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/client-recipes.test.mjs');
new Function('test','assert','settleMutation','sentResult','matchRead','otherwiseAll',recipes.slice(recipes.indexOf('for (const state')))(test,assert,settleMutation,sentResult,matchRead,otherwiseAll);
test('base-commit untouched starter client is absent from all recognized wave10 guest client forms',()=>{
  const prefix='snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/starter/';
  const old=source('f9e40ef515',prefix+'web/client.ts');
  const current=src(prefix+'web/client.ts'),previous=src(prefix+'previous/web-client.ts');
  const journal=text=>text.replace('import { webDevice } from \"snapback4/web\";\\n','').replace('device: webDevice(), ','').replace(', device: webDevice()','');
  for(const recognized of [current,previous,journal(current),journal(previous)]) assert.notEqual(old,recognized);
  console.log('Unmodified base starter client matches none of adapt.matches_client candidates.');
});
test('u16 frontend overflow is silently dropped from range validation',()=>{
  const backend=65535,frontend=backend===65535?undefined:backend+1;
  const selected=[backend,frontend].filter(p=>p"'!==undefined);
  assert.equal(selected.some(p=>p<65535||p>65535),false);
  assert.equal(frontend??5173,5173);
  console.log('"'init --web --port-range 65535-65535 validates then falls back to frontend port 5173.');
});

codex
The probes confirmed that an untouched starter from the base commit is no longer recognized by `init --adapt`, and a module-only project skips upgrade qualification. The form observer also accepted a background write before any click. The focused settlement tests passed, including early receipts, concurrent IDs, thrown admission, and explicit `waitSeconds: 0`.

   664	are reported and never invalidate valid product types. No store adoption, sync
   665	or authority change is introduced.
   666	
   667	Upgrade takes the old installed CLI's check snapshot before installation, then
   668	invokes the newly installed CLI's check after pinning. It preserves the package
   669	manager's dependency sections and ordinary recovery. A compiler refusal after a
   670	successful install returns nonzero with a message distinguishing installation
   671	from qualification; it never rolls back the packages silently. Without a schema,
   672	upgrade reports package pins only. Exact apps receive one rebuild pointer:
   673	`bun exact.mjs snapback --rebuild` (HS owns the command and device handshake).
   674	`guide changes` packages the release card; its version is bound to the compiler
   675	version and its factual nullable-count examples are covered by the guide tests.
   676	Nullable numeric aggregate writes offer `EXPR ?? 0` as an authored choice, not
   677	auto-apply: hidden keys remain null, authorized missing count groups return zero.
   678	
   679	The witness uses the declared persona independently of a guest-only sign-in
   680	screen by selecting createApp's normal persona option under the explicit
   681	witness flag. It still verifies the actual viewer before writing. Custom apps
   682	retain their visible persona picker; a missing persona app/picker refuses. No persona
   683	works against serve. Default starter examples use guest sign-in; `--as alice`
   684	on a starter that declares alice also works.
   685	
   686	A visible form proof is opt-in with `--field LABEL --value TEXT --button LABEL`.
   687	It fills the labelled control, presses the labelled button and observes that
   688	click's matching durable mutation through the registered app client. Before
   689	clicking it arms a fresh observer: an existing sent status or row cannot satisfy
   690	this proof. It waits for terminal sent and asserts the configured args when
   691	provided; failed or timed-out custody is nonzero. No second SDK mutation is
   692	injected. The ordinary SDK-only witness remains available without these flags.
   693	React Native web uses keyboard events (`pressSequentially`) to settle state;
   694	buttons use their accessible labels, not testID. Registration is flag-gated and
   695	cleanup restores its instrumentation. Starters label their field, render write
   696	status above the list, and supply a favicon.
   697	
   698	Port selection precedence is explicit --port/--web-port, explicit --port-range,
   699	then the complete PORT_LO/PORT_HI environment pair, then existing defaults.
   700	Ranges are validated before writing any files, and every selected port lies in
   701	them. The client URL, backend npm launcher, frontend dev/preview launcher,
   702	README and witness script share those selections. Later runtime URL overrides
   703	remain supported; the init range is a scaffold input, not an authority rule.
   704	HS owns the Vite Node declarations; DX2 adds no parallel type shim.
   705	
   706	Expo's web client and README derive their device-versus-journal choice from the
   707	same compiled sync detection. Its smoke helper uses the schema's password,
   708	guest or persona sign-in (password only on prime, with a witness-supplied test
   709	credential; it never puts a secret in source). The helper is inert in ordinary
   710	launches and other segments and waits for restoration. The native device and
   711	normal identity custody remain unchanged.
   712	
   713	Ordinary durable React `run()` owns an onWrite observer before admission and
   714	waits for sent/failed, independently of read subscribers and mounted effects.
   715	Early settlement cannot be missed and concurrent calls match only their own

NOT READY

Reviewed `a4b4900270` against `f9e40ef515`, including §9 and all changed DX2 surfaces.

**MATERIAL findings**

1. **Untouched wave-9 starters stop adapting.** The new persona code changes the [web client template](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/starter/web/client.ts:14), but the retained previous template predates wave 9. The base commit’s untouched client matches neither candidate in [adapt’s byte comparison](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/adapt.rs:144), so adaptation silently skips the screen/client. Changing that project to password identity or replacing its schema leaves incompatible starter code. Changed screen bytes create the same recognition problem. The new adaptation test only scaffolds with the new compiler.

2. **Module-only projects bypass upgrade qualification.** [upgrade.js:15](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/packages/snapback4/bin/upgrade.js:15) recognizes only `snapback/schema.q`; existing project loading supports other `.q` files. A project containing `snapback/notes.q` installs successfully without either compiler check, losing both upgrade comparison and post-install refusal detection. An in-memory execution confirmed zero check invocations and exit status 0.

3. **The visible-form proof can accept an unrelated background write.** [witness-web.ts:32](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/witness-web.ts:32) claims the first matching mutation after arming. Arming and clicking are separate browser operations at [witness-web.js:156](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/packages/snapback4/bin/witness-web.js:156). A timer/background mutation with matching arguments can settle between them; a broken button then still passes. The probe produced `sent` before any click. Fresh arming excludes old status, but does not establish §9’s claim that the receipt belongs to the click. Tests need an unrelated-write control.

4. **Frontend overflow escapes the selected port range.** In [init.rs:82](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init.rs:82), backend port `65535` makes `checked_add(1)` return `None`. Validation drops that missing frontend, then line 142 substitutes `5173` for web or `8081` for Expo. Consequently, `--port-range 65535-65535` writes launchers outside the range instead of refusing. The existing invalid-range tests miss this boundary.

**Minor findings**

- [starter.rs:339](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/init/starter.rs:339) misses the Todos README’s actual wrapping, `web uses the\nwasm device`. With an online-only Todos schema, the generated client uses the journal while the opening still claims wasm.
- [CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md:3182](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w10-dx2/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md:3182) still describes truncated piped guide output.

The three focused settlement/read-helper cases passed against immutable source in memory. Counterexample probes confirmed the findings above. The known emitter-format assertion mismatch is excluded from this verdict. No files were edited or pushed; no Cargo or heavyweight suite ran.
